Course structure AC dated 28.03.2025 - additional agenda BSH dept pg 11

# Syllabus common for B Tech/ MechaniMBA Tech (all programs), can use common question paper.

English Communication - This course focuses on introducing the students to the basics of English language and formal communication, which requires to be assessed continuously throughout the semester.

Essential Electronics Practices - This course empowers students to gather practical knowledge and skills using Internal Continues Assessment (ICA) throughout the semester.

Constitution of India - This course focuses on making the students aware of the Constitution of India, which is taught and assessed throughout the semester.

* Term Work = Viva / Presentations assignments quizeyclass participation/case study/mini project/project proposal & review 1/review 2/progress review report etc. The Term Work components are not exceeded 40% of the total ICA marks.

# Syllabus common for B Tech (Mechanical) and BTI (Mechanical), can use common question paper.

Machine Shop-I - course is a practice-oriented course which requires students to build jobs using different materials and machining processes. Evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments based on the jobs prepared by the students.

# Syllabus common for B Tech (Mechanical) and BTI (Mechanical), can use common question paper.

Industrial Applications of HVAC - This course imparts knowledge about HVAC design, control systems and plant equipment selection. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments of assignments and lab work.

Machine Shop-H - course is a practice-oriented course. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments based on the jobs prepared by the students.

*Course to be selected from the basket of Department Electives III/IV. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

*Course to be selected from a common open elective basket of interdisciplinary technical courses offered by different departments. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

*Course to be selected from a common open elective basket of interdisciplinary courses offered by different departments. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

Interpersonal Skills - This 1 credit practical course requires rigorous formative and summative Internal assessment continuously, to ensure the students have acquired specific interpersonal skills, thereby eliminating the need for term end assessment.

# Syllabus common for B Tech (Mechanical) and MBA Tech (Mechanical), can use common question paper.

Industrial Applications of HVAC - This course imparts knowledge about HVAC design, control systems and plant equipment selection. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments of assignments and lab work.

Machine Shop-II - course is a practice-oriented course. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments based on the jobs prepared by the students.

Course to be selected from the basket of Department Electives III/IV. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

Course to be selected from a common open elective basket of interdisciplinary technical courses offered by different departments. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

Course to be selected from a common open elective basket of interdisciplinary courses offered by different departments. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

Interpersonal Skills - This 1 credit practical course requires rigorous formative and summative Internal assessment continuously, to ensure the students have acquired specific interpersonal skills, thereby eliminating the need for term end assessment.

# Syllabus common for B Tech (Mechanical) and MBA Tech (Mechanical), can use common question paper.
**Industrial Applications of HVAC - course imparts knowledge about HVAC design, control systems, and plant equipment selection. Because it is entirely industry-based, evaluation is completed in NUE mode at the end of the semester.
Machine Shop-II - course is a practice-oriented course. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments based on the jobs prepared by the students.
Course to be selected from the basket of Department Electives III/IV. Scheme will be as per the course requirement.

. SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

Program: _ B.Tech. (Mechanical Engineering) Semester : VIII

Course: — Numerical Methods Code: BIME08013
Teaching Scheme Evaluation Scheme
Lecture | Practical | Tutorial Eeteneal Santini E Term End

per per per Credit As per Institute (TEE)
week) | week) week) nang Theory (3 Hrs, 70
(50 marks) Marks)
3 0 2 4 Scaled to 30 marks Scaled to 70 marks

Pre-requisite: Fundamental Knowledge of Calculus and Algebra.
Objectives:

1. To provide knowledge of numerical approximation theory in the process of
solving mathematical problem.

2. Inculcate the idea of convergence analysis of numerical methods associated with
numerical computation.

Course Outcomes:
After successful completion of this course, students will be able to
1. Solve algebraic, transcendental and differential equations using numerical
methods.
2. Implement appropriate techniques of numerical differentiation and integration
in solving relevant problems. .
3. Apply interpolation and curve fitting techniques to discrete numerical data.
4. Estimate and analyse errors in the numerical solution of mathematical

problems.
Detailed Syllabus: ( per session plan )
Unit | Description Duration
1, Errors in Numerical Computation: 5

Types of Errors, Analysis & Estimation of Errors, General Error
Formula, Errors in Taylor’s Series for Approximation of Functions.

2. Roots of Equations:
Bisection method, False position method, Newton-Raphson 6
method, Secant method, Applications.

3. Systems of Linear Algebraic equations:
Graphical Method, Cramer’s Rule, Matrix Inversion Method,
Substitution Methods, Gauss-Elimination method, Gauss-Jordan 10

method, Gauss- Jacobi Method, Gauss-Seidel method, Pitfalls &
improvement in each case, power method for eigenvalues, Jacobi’s
method to find the eigenvalues of a symmetric matrix.

4. Interpolation:
Forward, Backward and Central Differences, differences of a
polynomial, Newton’s Interpolation formulae, Stirling’s Central 6
Difference interpolation formula, Lagrange’s formula for unequal
intervals. -

ical/Sem-VIII/2018-19/Page 24

WOK
--- page 2 ---
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

5. Curve fitting:
Least square method for straight line and parabola, Method of 3
group averages.

6. Numerical differentiation:
Derivative using Newton’s forward difference and backward 6
difference interpolating formula, Maxima Minima of Tabulated
Function.

7s Numerical Integration: 3
The Trapezoidal rule, Simpson’s 1/3 rule, Simpson’s 3/8 rule.

8. Solution to Ordinary differential equations:
Taylor series method, Euler’s method, Modified Euler’s method, 6
Runge-Kutta method, Picard’s method of — successive
approximation.
Total 45

Text Books:

1. S.S. Sastry, Introduction to methods of numerical analysis, PHI, 5‘ Edition,
2013.

2. Saumyen Guha, Rajesh Srivatsava, Numerical Methods for Engineering and
Science, Oxford Higher education, 1* edition, 2010.
Reference Books: ;
1. Seven C. Chapra , Raymond P. Canale, Numerical Methods for Engineers, Tata
McGraw Hill , 7 Edition, , 2015.
2. S. R. K. Iyengar, R. K. Jain, Numerical Methods, New age international (p)
limited, publishers, 5'* edition, 2009.
3. _E. Balagurusamy, Numerical Methods, McGraw-Hill, 15t edition, 2017.

Objective :- To provide the understanding of properties and behavior of engineering materials,

different heat treatment processes of materials. This subject help for improvement, proper selection

and effective and optimum utilization of materials which is essential to satisfy for ever increasing

demand of society for modern living.

Prerequisite: Knowledge of Applied Science | and II

Unit Topics Duration

(Hrs)

1 MECHANICAL BEHAVIOR OF METALS

hardening on engineering behavior of material.

brittle and ductile fracture, Notch Effect.

theories of fatigue failure, S-N curve and its interpretation, Notch effect, surface By
effect, FY

constituent of phase diagram, Coring & dendritic segregation, Gibb’s, solid phase
rule, Eutectic, Peritectic & eutectoid system, Equilibrium diagrams for non -
} | ferrous alloys, Lever rule.

. Solidification and microstructure of different steels, Non equilibrium cooling of

steel,

Property variation with microstructure, classification and application of steel.

cast iron, Alloy cast iron

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by one

report on the Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of the
concerned teacher and submit the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester V.

1, Relevance and importance of the topic

Objective: To provide the understanding of large system of equations, nonlinearities and
complicated geometric that is often impossible to solve analytically. Numerical Methods are
most part designed for implementation on computers. Numerical methods is to reduce higher
mathematics to basic arithmetic operation, they get at the nut and bolts of some obscure topics.
Enhanced understanding and insight can result alternative perspective.

Prerequisite : Applied Maths I, II, & II

Detailed Syllahus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Roots of Equations — (including relevant engineering applications)
Bracketing Methods — The Bisection method, The False position method. 2

Open Methods - The Newton-Raphson method, The Secant method.

2 | Syscems of Linear Algebraic equations-(including relevant engineering

applications)

Gauss-Elimination method — Technique, pitfalls, improvement. Gauss- 4
Jordan method. LU decomposition and matrix inversion. Gauss-Seidel

method.

{3 | Curve fitting -(including relevant engineering applications)
} Interpolation — Newton’s divided difference, Lagrange Interpolating

polynomials. Approximation - Least square approximation technique, *
linear regression.

4 | Numerical differentiation-(including relevant engineering applications) 3
Methods based on interpolation and finite differences.

5 | Numerical Integration-(including relevant engineering applications) 3

The Trapezoidal rule, Simpson’s 1/3 rule, Simpson’s 1/8 rule

6 | Solution to Ordinary differential equations (including relevant
éngineering applications)
\ ‘Yaylor series method, Picard’s method of successive approximation. 3
' ange — Kutta methods — Euler’s method, Euler’s predicator-corrector
method, Range-Kutta method of second order.

7 | Optimization - (including relevant engineering applications and
transportation problems) 1
Goiden-section Search Method

Property variation with microstructure, classification and application
of steel.

Objectives: To provide the understanding of importance of manufacturing for the mankind and to know
the types manufacturing processes and evaluation of that processes

Prerequisite: Practical Knowledge of Mechanical Workshop First year

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration (Hrs)

1 | Metal Casting : 10
Pattern Making:- Types of pattern allowances, colour oding
Foundary:- Moulding sands, type properties, preparation and testing of sand,
core boxes, Core making ,type and manufacturing of core gating system-
runner, risers, Moulding processes, shell moulding, CO moulding investment
casting, Die casting, centrifugal casting and continuous casting , Defects in
casting.

2 | Joining process: 10
Surface preparation and Various Joints, classification of welding processes
Arc Welding :- Theory, Submerged Arc Welding, Gas -Metal Arc Welding,
Gas Tungsten Arc Welding.
Electron beam Welding Ultrasonic welding, laser bean welding
,| Resistance Welding:- Spot, Seam and Projection weld process.
} Gas welding. Soldering, Brazing.
Use adhesives for joining. Welding of various metals, rebuilding hard facing,
characteristics of good weld, defects, weldability.

3 Machining Process:- 12
Construction and working, operation of lathe, Screw cutting, Attachments and
accessories of centre lathe, Types of tools, cutting speed, depth of cut and
machining time, Capstan and Turret lathe .
NC, CNC and Machining Centre.
Milling Machines:-Types of Milling machines, Tools and operations on
milling machine. Different attachments(including dividing heads) and work

’ hot g devices.
B ine and Broaching:- Classification of boring machines, horizontal and
vertical boring machine, types of broaching machines, advantages, limitations
and applications of broaching.

4 | Drilling and Grinding Machines:-Drilling Machines-Types of Machines, 08
types of drilling operations such as drilling, boring, reaming, spot facing,
Counter boring and sinking, tapping. Drill speed and feeds.
Grinding Machines:- types and operations on grinding machines, Grinding
wheel selection and specification, dressing and truing of wheel. Super
finishing processes like lapping and honing.

Total Pies 40

Objectives: To provide the understanding of mechanics of fluid machinery by evaluaticn and analysis.

Prerequisite: Knowledge of Applied Physics I,[] and Engineering Mechanics

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration (Hrs)
1 Prime Movers :-Impact of jets, Theory of impulse and reaction machines. 08

Pelton, Francis and Kaplan turbines, their construction,
analysis, draft tubes, Specific speed, unit quantities, characteristics and
governing.

2 Centrifugal pumps :- Basic Theory, classification, construction, 08
operation, characteristics,
NPSH and cavitations in pumps.

3 Reciprocating Pump :- Basic theory, construction, operation, 10
Slip, Indicator diagram.
Rotary Pumps :- Basic theory, types, construction and
variable delivery pumps
} Other water lifting devices (Theory, Construction and operation)
(a) Air lift pump.
(b) Jet Pump.
(c) Hyd. Ram.

4 Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD) : Basic Definition, 07
Applications of CFD in the area of research & Industry Comparison of
Experimental Fluid Dynamics and
Computational Fluid Dynamics, Importance of Governing
Equations and the physical meaning of the involved terms.
| Equation of continuity, equation of motion & energy

’ ‘ ppalanie equation in Cartesian & cylindrical polar coordinates.

5 Hydrostatic systems, their function, components and application such as 07
Hydraulic press, lift, crane and fluid drive for machine tools. Intensifier
and accumulator.
Hydrokinetic systems : Fluid couplings and torque converter.

Total 40

Text Books:

1) Bansal R. K; Fluid Mechanics & Fluid Machines; Laxmi, 2005.
2) Jagdishlal,” Hydraulic Machines”, Metropolitan Book Co.2000.
--- page 7 ---
Recommended Books:

1) Som and Biswas; Fluid Mechanics and machinery; TMH Delhi, 2003.

2) Computational Fluid Dynamics — Basics and Applications by J D Anderson — Mc Hill
International Publications, 1995.

3) Computational Fluid Dynamics — Sen Gupta Tapan K. 2004.

Internet reference: As given by the faculty.
Term work consists of the following:

1) Minimum two class tests.
2) Assignments given by the faculty based on above syllabus.
3) Minimum 8 practical’s from the list.

Practical list:-
At least seven exercises based on the following.

1) Trial/study of Pelton turbine.

2) Trial/study of Francis turbine.

3) Trial/study of Kaplan Turbine.

4) Trial/study of centrifugal pump.

5) Trial/study of reciprocating pump.

6) Trial/study of Axial flow pump.

7) Study of multistage pump.

8) Trial/study of Hydraulic Ram.

9) Study of Hydrostatic components systems.

10) Study of Hydrostatic systems.

11) Study of Hydrokinetic systems.

12) Study of Cavitation phenomena.

13) Study of governing of Impulse Turbines.

14) Study of governing of reaction turbines.

15) Study of Special pumps (Air lift pump/jet pump)

16) Formulation of problem concerning the fluid flow in the vessel with any commercial code
available like CFX, FLUENT, PHOENIX.

Objective: To provide the understanding of basics of Machines and mechanisms. To understand
the motion analysis of mechanisms and study of basic machine components like flywheel, belt,
ropes and chains. Understanding of development of mechanism for simple motions.

Prerequisite: Knowledge of Applied Physics I,II and Engineering Mechanics

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration (Hrs) |
1 Kinematics: Basics(Mechanism and Machines, Rigid and resistant 12

body, Link), Classification Kinematics pairs (Lower pairs and higher
pairs ,Form closed pairs and force closed pairs), Based on relative
motion permitted such as revolute, prismatic, cam, helical Globular.
Grubler’s criterion, constraint, Unconstrained based on Grubler’s
criteria. Limitations of Grubler’s Criteria.

Inversion of chain: mechanisms based on uniform, non-uniform, SHM,
Non-SHM property. Limiting and dead positions, quick return
property, applications. Four bar chain , Single slider crank chain,
Double slider crank chain.

Special Mechanisms: Straight line generating Mechanisms: Paucellier’s
and Hart’s exact Straight Line Generating Mechanisms, Watt’s
Approximate straight line mechanics, Grass-hopper mechanism.
Pantograph, Hook joint- single and double, Steering gear mechanisms —
Ackerman, Davis.

2 Velocity and Acceleration analysis : (Graphical approach) 08
Velocity analysis:- instantaneous centre of rotation method, relative
velocity method, rubbing velocities at joints, mechanical advantage .
Vélocity and Acceleration analysis:- relative method including pairs
‘__| involving, Corioli’s acceleration.

3 | Static and dynamic force analysis:- slider crank mechanisms 06
neglecting mass of connecting rod and crank, Engine force analysis,
Turning moment on crank shaft.. Flywheel and its applications,
Fluctuation in energy, function of flywheel, estimating inertia of
flywheel for reciprocating prime movers and machines.

Belt, Ropes:- Types of belts, velocity ratio, slip & creep of belt, length 07
of belt for open & cross systems, law of belting, dynamic analysis-
4 | driving tensions, centrifugal tension, initial tension, condition of
maximum power transmission.
Chains :- Types of chains, chordal action, variation in velocity ratio,
chain length. ;

\
\

Vrs
--- page 9 ---
Introduction to Kinematic Synthesis:-Types of Synthesis, scale factor 07
for input and output motion, Chebyshev spacing of precision points,

Two Position Synthesis, Three position synthesis, Bloch’s synthesis

method, Synthesis of slider crank Mechanism.

Total 40

\
\

Text Books:

1) P.L. Ballaney, ’ Theory of Machines and Mechanisms” Khanna Publishing, New Delhi, 24m
Edition, 2005.
2) J.E.Shigley,” Theory of Mechanisms and Machines”, University Press, 3“ Edition, 2003.

Recommended Books:

1) Ratan S.S,” Theory of Machines ’Tata Mcgrew Hill, New Delhi, 3" Edition, 2009.
2) Ghosh and Malick,” Theory of Mechanisms and Machines”, East West Press Pvt. Ltd,
1988.

Internet reference: As given by the faculty.
Term work consists of the following:

1) Minimum two class tests.
2) Assignments given by the faculty based on above syllabus.
3) Minimum 6 practicals from the list.

Practical list:-

1) Study of various types of mechanisms and their inversions

2) To draw velocity diagram using Instantaneous centre method.

3) To draw velocity diagram using relative velocity method.

4) To draw velocity and acceleration diagram for simple mechanism.

5) To draw velocity and acceleration diagram for mechanisms involving Corioli’s
component.

6) To find Slip of belt.

7) To study two and three position synthesis.

8) To study synthesis for slider crank Mechanism.

the engineering systems.

Unit Topics Duration (Hrs)
1 Thyristors and their Applications: Principle of operation of SCR, Two 04

Transistor Analogy of SCR, Triggering circuits of SCR, and its
application as controlled rectifier, Introduction to three phase rectifier
using SCR, DIAC, TRIAC

2 Invertors, Choppers: Commutation circuits, Series and parallel inverter, 05
choppers- step-up, step-down, Morgan’s, jones’s Single phase to three

3 Solid state control of DC and AC Motors : Introduction to motor, speed 04
control of DC motor, over voltage and over current protection , speed
H control of AC motor using thyristor.

4} Operational Amplifier and Timer Circuits: Introduction and basic 03
application of IC 741. Monostable and Astable multivibrator using [C555
5 Introduction to Microprocessor: Booleans algebra, Symbol and truth 04
table Logic gates, universal gates, Introduction to 8085 microprocessor,

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

B.Tech. (All Branches) Trimester V

Sub : Seminar — I Code : S-I

Periods per week Guided Library Work | 4 Hrs.

Evaluation Scheme | Report (T/W) warks

Objective:

To develop ability to collect information and prepare a report for presentation on a
technical topic.

Prerequisite:

Basic Discipline, subjects up to Trimester IV.

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by one of the
teachers teaching the Trimester V subjects. The students will prepare the detailed report on the
Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of the concerned teacher and submit
the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester V.

The report will cover the following:

1. Relevance and importance of the topic

2. Basic Principles / Concepts / Definitions

3. Details of development — Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams
; 4. Applications — Advantages and Limitations.
} 5. Discussion of related issues.

6. Future scope

7. References.

Objectives: Computer graphics has made computers easier to interact with, and bewier for
understanding and interpreting many types of geometrical data which helps students to
understand the concepts of 2D and 3D modeling and develops the ability in 3D modeling.

Prerequisite: Knowledge of CC++ and basics of mathematics.

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration (Hrs)
bT Introduction:-application area, input & output devices, video 04

display devices, refresh CRT, Raster Scan display, Color CRT,
Monitor, Coordinate representation.

2 Algorithm for 2-D Drawing:-Out put characteristics ,aspect ratio, 12
Aliasing and anti aliasing, Line Drawing Algorithms, Bresnham’s
Algorithm, Circle Generation Algorithm, Midpoint circle
Algorithm, Ellipse generation Algorithm, Midpoint Ellipse
Algorithm, Area filling, Scan line polygon filling, Inside outside
test, Boundary fill Algorithm

3 2-D Geometric Transformation:-Window and view port; their 07
relationship, World coordinates, Normalized device coordinates,
Basic transformation, rotation, scaling, other transformation,
reflection and shear, composite transformation. 2-D viewing and

clipping. _

4 3-D Concepts:- 3-D display methods, parallel and perspective 02
projection, Depth Cuing, 3-D Transformation, Translation,
Rotation and Scaling, Other transformation, reflection and shear,
composite transformation,3-D viewing and clipping.

5 ; Hidden Surface Elimination Methods:- Back face Detection, 04
j Depth or Z-Buffer Method, Scan Line Method, Area subdivision
Ly |_| method. :
6"! | Line Shading:- Illumination Model, Shading, Constant Intensity 05
Shading, Gouraud Shading, Phong Shading, Halftoning, Ray
tracing.

Total 40

B.Tech(Mechanical & Civil Engineering) Trimester VI
Sub: STRENGTH OF MATERIALS Code:
; Lecture 5 hrs.
Periods per week Practical 2 Hrs.
Tutorial -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme Theory Examination i) 100 (Scaled down to 50)
Test 1 20
Term work/Pr./Oral 30

Objective: The aim of the syllabus is to make the students conversant with various types of
forces/loading and the resultant stresses there on to which mechanical components and
structures are subjected to. All engineering structures do deform under the action of loads to
which they are subjected. These deformations are expected to be within acceptable limits. The
structural member is not expected to exceed its deformation limits or fail or fracture under
normal operating conditions. Thus the structural member is expected to be sufficiently strong,
stiff and stable while in operation. The study of these parameters viz. The strength, stiffness
and stability of the member under loading will be dealt in this syllabus of Mechanics of
Deformable bodies more commonly referred to as Strength of Materials.

Prerequisite | Course in Engineering Mechanics, Statistics

Detailed syllabus

Units Topics Duration (Hrs.)

Stress and Strain: Strain, modulus of elasticity, modulus of
rigidity, bulk modulus, yield stress, ultimate stress, factor of safety,
shear stress, Poisson’s ratio, analysis of members made of
composite materials.

Shear Force and Bending Moment: Axial force, shear force and
bending moment diagrams for statically determinate beams
(excluding beams with internal hinges) for different types of
loading.

Simple Theory of Bending: Flexure formula for straight beams,
3 simple problems involving application of flexure formula, section 6
} | modulus, moment of resistance of a section.

Shear Stress in Beams: Distribution of shear stress across plane
q } sections used commonly for structural purposes.

Simple Theory of Torsion: Torsion of circular shafts — solid and
5 hollow, stresses in power transmission shafts (excluding shafts in 3
series and parallel).

Bending Moment Combined with Axial Loads:
6 Application to members subjected to eccentric loads, core of a 3
section, problems on chimneys involving lateral loads.

LP»
--- page 7 ---
Principal Stresses: General equations for transformation of stress,
stress on an oblique plane of a member subjected to General two
directional stress system. Principal planes and principal stresses,
maximum shear stress, Mohr’s circle.

Deflection of Beams: Deflection of cantilevers, simply supported
8 and overhanging beams using double integration and Macaulay’s 6
methods for different types of loading.

Struts: Struts subjected to axial loads, concept of buckling, Euler’s

4
? and Rankine’s formula for struts with different support conditions.
Thin Shells: Thin cylindrical and spherical shells, volumetric
10 . nr : ; : 4
strain, cylindrical shell with hemispherical ends.
Total 50
Text Books:

1. Mechanics of Materials by E. P. Popov, PHI(EEE) , 2000
2. Strength of Materials by Junnarkar and Dr. Adivi, Charotar Publications, 2010

Reference books:

Sub:- Theory of Machine II Code

Hours Marks

Work/PR/Oral

Objectives: To provide the understanding of basic mechanical components which is used in
mechanical systems Analysis and evaluation of these components like clutches, brakes, cams
and governors and gear will help the students to understand the systems like engine
compressors, boilers and designing of these systems.

Prerequisite: Knowledge of Applied Physics I,1] and Engineering Mechanics

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration (Hrs)

1 Friction Clutches- Pivot and Collar friction, Plate clutches, Cone 08
clutch, Centrifugal clutch, Torque transmitting capacity, Clutch
operating mechanisms.
Brakes and Dynamometers- Different types of brakes, Shoe
brakes, External and Internal shoe brakes, Block brakes, Band
brakes, Band and Block brakes, Braking torques, Different types of
absorption and transmission type dynamometers.

2 Cams and Followers- Types of cams and followers, Analysis of 08
standard motions to the follower, Determination of cam profiles for
given follower motions, Analysis of cams with specified contours-
circular arc cam, tangent cam, eccentric cam, Kinematically
equivalent system, Jump phenomenon, Introduction to Advanced
cam curves.

3 Governors- Function, Inertia and centrifugal type governors, 06
} Different types of centrifugal governors (Watt, Porter, Proell and
i Hartnell only), Controlling force analysis, Governor effort and
V ) governor power, sensitivity, stability, Isochronism and hunting,
Friction, Insensitiveness.

4 Gears and Gear trains- 12
Spur Gears-terminology of gearing, conjugate action, involute and
cycloidal profile, path of contact, arc of contact, contact ratio,
interference, undercutting, Methods to avoid interference and
undercutting, Rack shift, Effect of center distance variation, friction
between gear teeth, internal gears,
Helical Gears: Normal and transverse module, Torque transmitted
by helical gears on parallel shafts. Virtual number of teeth.
Spiral Gears- Spiral angle, shaft angle, Ege of spiral gears.

ATES sos

I> Ruse RAP
[26 Sees
{™s NMIMS
Mur i
--- page 9 ---
Worm and worm gears & Bevel Gears: Terminology, geometrical
relationships, applications and tooth forces. Torque transmitted.
Gear trains: Types of gear trains. Velocity ratio, Tooth load, torque
transmitted. Holding torque.

Gyroscope:-Principle, Gyroscopic couple, Effect of gyroscopic 06
couple on stability of an automobile, Gyroscopic effect on two
wheeler, stabilization of sea vessel.

Total 40

Text Books:

1) P.L. Ballaney, *Theory of Machines and Mechanisms” Khanna Publishing, New
Delhi. 24" Edition, 2005.

2) J.E.Shigley, ” Theory of Mechanisms and Machines ”,University Press, 3" Edition,
2003.

Recommended Books:

1) Ratan S.S,” Theory of Machines *Tata Mcgrew Hill, New Delhi. , 3 Edition, 2009.
2) Ghosh and Malick,” Theory of Mechanisms and Machines ”,East West Press Pvt.
Ltd, 1988.

Internet reference: As given by the faculty.
Term work consists of the following:

1) Minimum two class tests.
2) Assignments given by the faculty based on above syllabus.
3) Minimum 6 practical’s from the list.

Practical list:-

1. Study of various types of friction clutches and to measure torque transmitting
capacity.

2. Study of various types of brakes and dynamometers and to measure the power
transmitted

3. Study of various types of cam and follower systems and Verification of cam jump
phenomenon.

4. To draw cam profiles for various types of follower motions.

5. To determine the characteristics curve for centrifugal governor and to find its
coefficient of insensitiveness and stability.

6. Study of various types of gearboxes such as Industrial gear box, Synchromesh
gearbox, Differential gearbox, PIV gearbox.

7. To draw conjugate profile for any general type of gear tooth.

8. To generate involute gear tooth profile and to study the effect of undercutting and
rack shift using models.

9. To study epicyclic gear trains and to measure torques- torque transmitted and
holding torques.

Industrial Applications of HVAC - This course imparts knowledge about HVAC design, control systems and plant equipment selection. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments of assignments and lab work.

Machine Shop-II - course is a practice-oriented course. The evaluation is purely based on periodic assessments based on the jobs prepared by the students.
